州警告宠物主人接种 狂犬病 疫苗 - The Record Courier

State warns pet owners to vaccinate for rabies - The Record Courier
新闻简报内华达州农业部在里昂县、白松县和克拉克县的蝙蝠中检测到 狂犬病 后,发布了公共卫生警告。由于蝙蝠从 5 月到 10 月都很活跃,官员们强调给宠物接种 狂犬病 疫苗的重要性,狂犬病 是一种致命病毒,会影响包括狗、猫、牲畜和人类在内的哺乳动物。今年,三只蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测结果呈阳性,这凸显了预防性护理的必要性。内华达州法律规定,狗、猫和雪貂必须使用 狂犬病 疫苗接种,某些牲畜也必须使用 疫苗。敦促居民避免直接接触野生动物,特别是蝙蝠,并向地方当局报告可疑接触情况。动物疾病实验室继续进行年度监测,以追踪病毒并支持公共卫生应对措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 23 日。事件时间范围:内华达州蝙蝠从 5 月到 10 月都很活跃。
地点与对象
内华达州,特别是里昂县、白松县和克拉克县。受影响人群:居民、宠物主人及其宠物(狗、猫、牲畜)。
事件
内华达州农业部动物疾病实验室在蝙蝠中发现确诊的 狂犬病 病例。该事件是强调 疫苗接种 要求的公共卫生警告。
规模、影响与风险
自 2026 年初以来,三只蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性。狂犬病 是一种致命病毒,会影响包括宠物和人类在内的哺乳动物。通过 疫苗接种 和避免与野生动物接触来管理风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
内华达州法律要求狗、猫和雪貂接种 狂犬病 疫苗。年度监测继续发现阳性蝙蝠。建议公众就可疑接触情况联系动物控制或卫生部门。测试有助于追踪病毒的存在并告知公众反应。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

State warns pet owners to vaccinate for rabies - The Record Courier

News Brief
The Nevada Department of Agriculture has issued a public health warning after detecting rabies in bats from Lyon, White Pine, and Clark counties. With bats active from May through October, officials emphasize the importance of vaccinating pets against rabies, a deadly virus affecting mammals including dogs, cats, livestock, and humans. Three bats have tested positive for rabies this year, reinforcing the need for preventive care. Nevada law mandates rabies vaccinations for dogs, cats, and ferrets, with vaccines also available for certain livestock. Residents are urged to avoid direct contact with wildlife, particularly bats, and to report suspected exposures to local authorities. The Animal Disease Laboratory continues annual monitoring to track the virus and support public health responses.
Original source text (English)
🏠 About Us Submit News Newsletter Donate Subscribe Find a Paper News Crime Running Commentary Local Business Community Politics e-Edition Obituaries Opinion Sports Behind the Bench Special Sections Best Of 2022 Magazine 2023 Magazine 2024 Magazine 2025 Magazine Best of Contest Classifieds Jobs Real Estate Autos Legals 🏆 Remarkable Women Cutest Creatures $5,000 Sweepstakes e-Edition State warns pet owners to vaccinate for rabies Bats are common throughout Nevada, with their activity increasing between May and October. Nevada Department of Agriculture photo Staff Reports Thursday, July 23, 2026 Share this: Email | Facebook | X Rabies has been detected in bats captured in Lyon, White Pine and Clark counties that tested positive for rabies prompting a reminder from the Nevada Department of Agriculture for residents to protect themselves and their pets.“Rabies can be effectively controlled with proper vaccination,” said NDA State Veterinarian Dr. Peter Rolfe, DVM. “Keeping pets up to date on vaccines is the best way to protect both animals and the people around them.”The Department’s Animal Disease Laboratory confirms rabies in multiple bats from across the state every year, highlighting the importance of vaccination and vigilance. Since the beginning of the year, the laboratory has identified three bats from Lyon, White Pine and Clark counties that tested positive for rabies.Rabies is a deadly virus that can affect mammals, including dogs, cats, livestock and humans. In Nevada, bats are active from May through October and can enter homes or barns undetected. While bats are the most common source of transmission in the state, other wildlife can also carry the virus.“Annual monitoring in Nevada continues to identify bats that test positive for rabies, reinforcing the need for preventive care,” Animal Disease Laboratory supervisor added Laura Morrow,. “Testing helps us track where the virus is present and provide information to the public and animal health agencies, supporting their response to potential exposures.” Nevada law requires dogs, cats and ferrets to be vaccinated against rabies. Vaccines are also available for certain livestock species. Animal owners should work with their veterinarians to maintain an appropriate vaccination schedule.“Maintaining current rabies vaccinations for pets and avoiding direct contact with wildlife are critical steps in reducing risk,” said NDA Director J.J. Goicoechea. “Bats, in particular, should never be handled, as they can carry rabies even when no obvious signs of illness are present.”For more information on rabies prevention, visit the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention website at cdc.gov/rabies or contact the Southern Nevada Health District, Northern Nevada Public Health or your local veterinarian.Anyone who encounters a bat or other wildlife that may have rabies should contact their local animal control office, vector control or health department for assistance. Do not attempt to pick up the bat without guidance. Suspected rabies exposure or cases in animals should be reported to the NDA Animal Disease Laboratory at diseasereporting@agri.nv.gov or (775) 353-3709. 大理石镇之谜 - Shawangunk Journal

Mysteries of Marbletown - Shawangunk Journal
新闻简报7 月 4 日,马布尔敦 (Marbletown) 居民劳拉·谢恩·坎宁安 (Laura Shaine Cunningham) 从自家门廊捡起一只活蝙蝠,误以为是一片树叶,导致健康事故发生。尽管蝙蝠没有咬她,但阿尔斯特县卫生部仍将此次接触列为 狂犬病 暴露风险,因为蝙蝠唾液可以传播这种几乎 100% 致命的病毒。在延迟寻求 治疗 后,Cunningham 经卫生部门授权,在金斯顿 HealthAlliance 医院接受了七次注射 狂犬病 暴露后预防 (RPEP) 系列。这一事件凸显了即使没有被蝙蝠咬伤,与蝙蝠接触的严重风险,以及及时就医的重要性。2025 年,阿尔斯特县有 95 人需要类似的 狂犬病 疫苗接种。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-23。事件日期:7月4日(年份未指明,但上下文表明为近期)。
地点与对象
纽约州阿尔斯特县,特别是马布尔敦-罗森代尔地区。受影响的人:劳拉·谢恩·坎宁安 (Laura Shaine Cunningham),一位与蝙蝠有过接触的居民。
事件
已确认的蝙蝠接触事件需要 狂犬病 暴露后预防 (RPEP)。由该人本人举报。事件已确认,治疗 正在进行中。
规模、影响与风险
一个人暴露了。由于蝙蝠唾液中存在潜在的 狂犬病病毒 传播,因此风险较高。如果出现症状,狂犬病 几乎 100% 致命,但如果按计划给药,疫苗接种 则 100% 有效。2025 年,阿尔斯特县有 95 人需要 狂犬病 疫苗接种。
应对、状态与不确定性
调查和 治疗 正在进行中。该蝙蝠未经测试,因此 狂犬病 状态未知。在访问埃伦维尔急诊室的 疫苗 出现延误后,该患者正在金斯顿 HealthAlliance 医院接受七次 RPEP 系列注射。阿尔斯特县卫生部参与其中,并于凌晨 1 点获得了 治疗 的授权。来源是完整的原始报告。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mysteries of Marbletown - Shawangunk Journal

News Brief
On July 4th, a Marbletown resident, Laura Shaine Cunningham, experienced a potentially dangerous health incident after picking up a live bat from her porch, mistaking it for a leaf. Despite the bat not biting her, the Ulster County Department of Health classified the contact as a rabies exposure risk, as bat saliva can transmit the nearly 100% fatal virus. After a delay in seeking treatment, Cunningham underwent a seven-injection rabies postexposure prophylaxis (RPEP) series at HealthAlliance Hospital in Kingston, following authorization from the health department. The incident highlights the severe risk of bat contact, even without a bite, and the importance of prompt medical attention. In 2025, 95 people in Ulster County required similar rabies vaccinations.
Original source text (English)
Search PublicationsHomeNewsVideocastsOpinionsColumnsNewscastsCalendarBlotterObituariesPublic NoticesArchive 2006 – 2018SubscribeE-Newsletter Sign UpPrint Subscription RenewalsMake A ContributionAdvertiseAbout UsContact UsSubmit News TipFAQPrivacy / Terms of ServiceLog InSign Up Read More Support Shawangunk JournalFundraiser at $17,705 towards $25,000 goal 7/23/2026 •Nature & Environment Mysteries of Marbletown Nosferatu and Me – I Touched a Bat and Now I’m Sorry Opinion By Laura Shaine Cunningham CourtesyOf course it happened on a holiday. I woke up before dawn on the 4th of July and went out...Of course it happened on a holiday. I woke up before dawn on the 4th of July and went out to tidy my porch, and picked up a live bat, I mistook for a leaf on my wicker sofa. I let go of him fast but the potential horror and the painful suspense and treatment is ongoing. It turns out touching a bat is regarded by the Ulster County Department of Health is as dangerous as being bitten by one. Bats drool. And if rabid, their saliva carries the rabies virus. And if infected, almost no one survives rabies. There are recent news accounts of children dying after accidental contact with bats. One boy in Canada awoke with a bat spread out over his nose and mouth. He didn’t seem to be bitten, but he died. It turns out “contact” may be elusive to diagnose – bats have tiny teeth and you might not feel you were bitten. But as I’ve learned to my horror, you don’t have to be bitten at all – the mere contact can be deadly, as bats drool, and their saliva, if they are rabid, carries the lethal rabies virus. At first, my accidental contact didn’t seem that alarming. I’ve lived happily with the bats behind my shutters for 45 years as they would flutter out at dusk and eat thousands of mosquitoes...I knew they were there as they also left huge black piles of guano, which I swept off my porch onto my rose garden where it was excellent fertilizer. I would occasionally hear their faint chirps behind the shutters, and once my husband was hosing the clapboard above the shutters, and they flew out below his underarms and he didn’t know, but I watched the black cloud flee... Several years ago, I actually worried about the bats when they seemed to have disappeared during a known outbreak of “white nose” disease which was killing bats en masse. There were many articles and notices that we needed those bats to recover and survive, as they are important in the environmental chain of command. People were advised to install bat houses to help them out. My area, Marbletown-Rosendale, is famous for its huge cavern hibernaculum (cave where bats hibernate, forbidden for humans to enter during their winter hibernation period), the Walter Williams Preserve (also known as the "Rosendale Bat Cave"), an abandoned, former limestone cement mine that now serves as a designated winter habitat for federally protected endangered bats, including the Indiana bat. It was there in 2007-8, that the fungus infection White-nose syndrome (WNS) is a deadly fungal disease that devastated bat populations across the Northeast was first reported. Ulster County holds historical significance as one of the primary epicenters of the disease. Caused by the fungus Pseudogymnoascus destructans (Pd), it thrives in the cold, damp environments of caves and abandoned mines. Most of the bats died. So for several summers, I actually missed my bats and was sad when only a few tiny guano droppings would appear, but this year they seemed back to normal. For my own situation of having I thought a casual insignificant contact with a bat,, I checked online and read one post that stated the notorious intramuscular series of vaccinations shots were now down to a routine two regular injections, but now I know to my regret that is incorrect. I waited two days to go down to Ellenville ER and request the vaccinations, only to discover Ellenville doesn’t stock the vaccine, even though demand is high. The Ellenville nurses took down my information and filled out a Department of Health form, stressed that this had to be reported to the Ulster County Department of Health, and I’d best get the shots, asap! In our neck of the woods, the only hospital to give the shots is HealthAlliance in Kingston. The vaccinations must be administered where you have had the bite or contact, in Ulster County, so no dashing across the river. Ellenville and the urgent care offices send everyone to Kingston, where the HealthAlliance staff is not too happy about all the referrals....and Ulster hospital ER means long waits. At HealthAlliance they had to phone the Department of Health for a Human Rabies Authorization for RPEP, Human Rabies Postexposure Prophylaxis. As by then it was one a.m., I asked “won’t they be out or asleep? “We wake them up,” was the answer.Whoever the nurse reached, even at 1 a.m., approved me and right now, I'm in the middle of a series of seven injections, the first two being the painful intramuscular. I am in some danger apparently because I delayed going to the ER, and the risk of the bat drool carrying the rabies virus. If the disease starts after its incubation period which is usually long 4-8 weeks but can also be as short as a few days – the disease is nearly 100% fatal. The vaccination if given on schedule is 100% effective. And you must be vaccinated according to the Ulster County Department of Health if you even just touch a bat I’ve been getting out of the Health Alliance ER in the wee hours, which adds to the surreal mood. The nice nurses give me plenty of anecdotes and say a lot of people come in, many are from Ellenville for the vaccinations, including a young man who was feeding and playing with a raccoon until it bit him. My mistakes: First, according to Eve Walter, Ph.D. at the Ulster Department of Health, I should have collected the bat and brought him in for testing. If he tested negative, no vaccinations would have been needed, sparing me the pain and the county the expense. And I should not have delayed going to the ER on the off-chance my little bat was rabid – only 1% of our local bats carry the virus, but the nurse also asks if “he was acting normal?” “What is normal for a bat? I never found a bat on a sofa before. He was awfully passive and didn’t fly away when I let go." Was he sick or merely sleepy?I will know more in the next several weeks, but I feel you all should know there is no casual contact with a bat. In 2025, 95 people in Ulster County required rabies vaccinations. So far in 2026: 91. He can be as deadly as Nosferatu, or as he’s best known, Dracula. The only positive note is that the vaccinations are free.“You won’t be charged,” the night nurse called after me as I walked out into the dark of night.Laura Shaine Cunningham has written many plays and books, including A Place in the Country. Event

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

遭遇野生动物的增加引发了 CK 公共卫生预防提示 - CK News Today

Rise in wildlife encounters triggers CK Public Health precaution tips - CK News Today
新闻简报随着野生动物接触蝙蝠和浣熊的数量增加,查塔姆-肯特公共卫生部门发布了预防提示,导致更多的人需要 狂犬病 暴露后预防 (PEP)。虽然人类 狂犬病 病例很少见,并且可以通过及时的医疗干预进行治疗,但官员强调,避免与野生动物直接接触是最好的保护。卫生部门建议居民远远地欣赏动物,不要触摸或喂养它们,确保宠物接种疫苗,并为生病或受伤的野生动物寻求专业帮助。那些被蝙蝠咬伤、抓伤或直接接触蝙蝠的人应彻底清洗该区域并立即就医,同时报告潜在的 CKPH 暴露情况以进行后续处理。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 24 日。活动时间表未指定,但据描述最近遭遇野生动物的次数有所增加。
地点与对象
查塔姆-肯特,安大略省,加拿大。广大公众,特别是居民和儿童。
事件
查塔姆-肯特公共卫生局 (CKPH) 报告称,可预防的野生动物与蝙蝠和浣熊的接触有所增加,导致 狂犬病 暴露后预防 (PEP) 治疗 数量增加。该事件被确认为公共卫生预防通知。
规模、影响与风险
人类 狂犬病 病例很少见,但通过及时干预是可以治疗的。越来越多的人需要 PEP。明确指出该风险可以通过避免与野生动物直接接触来预防。
应对、状态与不确定性
CKPH 建议公众远离野生动物并避免接触。预防措施包括不接触野生动物、给宠物接种疫苗以及接触后就医。潜在暴露应报告给 CKPH 进行评估。来源是完整的原始报告,没有未解决的信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rise in wildlife encounters triggers CK Public Health precaution tips - CK News Today

News Brief
Chatham-Kent Public Health has issued precautionary tips following an increase in wildlife encounters with bats and raccoons, leading to more people requiring Rabies Post-Exposure Prophylaxis (PEP). While human rabies cases are rare and treatable with timely medical intervention, officials emphasize that avoiding direct contact with wildlife is the best protection. The health unit advises residents to admire animals from a distance, never touch or feed them, ensure pets are vaccinated, and seek professional help for sick or injured wildlife. Those bitten, scratched, or in direct contact with a bat should wash the area thoroughly and seek immediate medical attention, while also reporting potential exposures to CKPH for follow-up.
Original source text (English)

"Even animals that appear healthy, friendly, injured, or orphaned can carry rabies. We encourage residents to admire wildlife from a distance and never attempt to touch, feed, or handle wild animals."Officials noted that bats and raccoons are among the wildlife species most commonly associated with rabies exposures in Ontario and remind individuals to take the following precautions:Never touch, handle, or feed wild animals.Keep a safe distance from bats, raccoons, and other wildlife.Teach children to avoid approaching or touching wild animals.Ensure pets are up to date on their rabies vaccinations.Seek assistance from trained animal control or wildlife professionals if an animal appears sick, injured, or abandoned.CKPH said people who have been bitten or scratched by a wild animal, or who have had direct contact with a bat, should immediately wash the affected area thoroughly with soap and water and seek medical attention as soon as possible.Potential exposures should also be reported to CKPH for assessment and follow-up, officials noted."Protecting yourself and your family starts with keeping a respectful distance from wildlife," added Standel. View five news elements
Time
Published on July 24, 2026. Event timeframe not specified, but the rise in wildlife encounters is described as recent.
Location and population
Chatham-Kent, Ontario, Canada. General public, with emphasis on residents and children.
Event
Chatham-Kent Public Health (CKPH) reported an increase in preventable wildlife encounters with bats and raccoons, leading to a rise in Rabies Post-Exposure Prophylaxis (PEP) treatments. The event is confirmed as a public health precautionary notice.
Scale, impact and risk
Cases of human rabies are rare but treatable with timely intervention. A growing number of people have required PEP. The risk is explicitly stated as preventable by avoiding direct contact with wildlife.
Response, status and uncertainty
CKPH is advising the public to admire wildlife from a distance and avoid contact. Precautions include not touching wild animals, keeping pets vaccinated, and seeking medical attention after exposure. Potential exposures should be reported to CKPH for assessment. The source is a full original report with no unresolved information.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

蝙蝠落在 Vista 餐厅的餐桌上后,卫生官员发出 狂犬病 警告 - NBC 7 San Diego

Health officials warn of rabies after bat lands on table at Vista restaurant - NBC 7 San Diego
新闻简报7 月 19 日,一只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠落在 Vista 的 Yellow Deli 餐厅的桌子上,圣地亚哥县卫生官员向居民发出警告。这只蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测结果呈阳性,但尚未有人类接触的报道。官员们敦促任何可能接触过蝙蝠的人立即就医,因为如果不治疗,狂犬病 几乎 100% 致命。这标志着该县今年出现了第 22 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,人类与蝙蝠的互动也有所增加。卫生团队正在努力识别潜在暴露并提出建议,强调 暴露后预防 的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 24 日;事件发生在下午 1 点。
地点与对象
维斯塔,圣地亚哥县,加利福尼亚州,美国;黄色熟食餐厅;一家人吃晚饭。
事件
一家人正在吃饭时,一只蝙蝠落在餐馆的桌子上,其 狂犬病 检测结果呈阳性;卫生官员发出警告。
规模、影响与风险
圣地亚哥县今年第 22 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠;尚未报告人类接触;如果不治疗,狂犬病 的死亡率接近 100%。
应对、状态与不确定性
官员试图联系家人;调查潜在的暴露;如果需要,建议 暴露后预防;bat 监测 正在进行。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health officials warn of rabies after bat lands on table at Vista restaurant - NBC 7 San Diego

News Brief
San Diego County health officials are warning residents after a rabid bat landed on a table at Yellow Deli Restaurant in Vista on July 19. The bat tested positive for rabies, though no human contacts have been reported. Officials are urging anyone who may have touched the bat to seek immediate medical care, as rabies is nearly 100% fatal if untreated. This marks the 22nd rabid bat in the county this year, with increased human-bat interactions noted. Health teams are working to identify and advise potential exposures, emphasizing the importance of post-exposure prophylaxis.
Original source text (English)

By Pranati Kotamraju • Published July 23, 2026 • Updated on July 24, 2026 at 12:59 pm Log in or create a free profile to save articlesBOOKMARKER 0:00Pause0:00 / 0:58UnmuteSubtitlesEnglishNoneSettingsShareToggle Fullscreenestimates that any given night. Get this. There are as many as NBC Universal, Inc. Local health officials say a bat that tested positive for rabies landed on the table of a restaurant while a family was eating. Now, they have a warning for anyone who may have touched it. San Diego County officials are looking for anyone who may have come in direct contact with a rabid bat after it flew into a Vista restaurant on Sunday. The incident occurred at 1 p.m. on July 19 at the Yellow Deli Restaurant, when a family was eating dinner and a bat landed on their table. Another diner picked up the bat using a towel and brought it to the Humane Society in Oceanside. Stream San Diego News for free, 24/7, wherever you are with NBC 7. WATCH HERE After being brought to the county’s public health lab, the bat tested positive for rabies, but as of Thursday, there have been no reports of people coming into direct contact with the bat, according to the county. Health officials warn any individuals who may have come in direct contact with the bat to call their health care provider immediately. "Rabies has nearly a 100% fatality rate if treatment is not obtained prior to symptoms starting. So you need to seek care before symptoms start, or there is no treatment for rabies, unfortunately," said Emily Trumbull, a veterinarian for the county of San Diego’s Epidemiology Unit. This is the 22nd bat to test positive for rabies in San Diego County this year, as there has been an increased number of human and bat interactions this year--- but it doesn’t directly correlate to more bats having rabies, Trumbull said. “We work closely with animal health partners throughout the county to monitor for rabies among a variety of species,” Trumbull said. “We test animals that may be suspicious for rabies, or that could be putting someone at high risk because of the type of exposure they had to these animals. Animals are under surveillance for rabies in the county of San Diego.” Local San Diego Jul 16 San Diego Comic-Con events, freebies and more things to do without a badge North Park 13 hours ago Elderly Kensington residents shaken by string of break-ins Exposure to rabies means that one was either bitten or had saliva exposure to an animal infected with rabies that has entered your eyes, nose, or mouth, or any other mucous membrane, Trumbull said. “Bats are protected wildlife, and they're very important to our ecosystem,” Trumbull said. “So it's really important to never touch a bat. It's important to respect the space of wildlife and stay a safe distance away from them. If the bat is available for rabies testing in those instances, then it can help inform whether you need post-exposure prophylaxis care.” Currently, officials are attempting to contact a family that had the bat fly onto their table. Their main concern is to explain to the family the risk of rabies transmission and see if they qualify for post-exposure prophylaxis care. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bat that dropped onto table at SoCal restaurant had rabies, officials say - Los Angeles Times

News Brief
A bat that dropped onto a table at a Southern California restaurant has been confirmed to have rabies by health officials. The incident occurred at an unspecified restaurant in the region, though no human cases or exposures have been reported. Authorities have verified the bat's rabies status, but further details about potential contact tracing, testing, or public health measures remain undisclosed. The event highlights the ongoing risk of rabies transmission from bats to humans in California, though the immediate public health impact appears limited.

内华达州敦促居民为宠物接种 狂犬病 疫苗 - FOX5 Vegas

Nevada urges residents to vaccinate pets against rabies - FOX5 Vegas
新闻简报自2026年初以来,内华达州农业部在里昂、白松和克拉克县发现了三只狂犬病阳性蝙蝠,敦促居民为他们的宠物接种狂犬病疫苗。州兽医博士强调,正确的狂犬病可以有效控制狂犬病,保护动物和人类。这种致命病毒会影响狗、猫、牲畜和人类,其中蝙蝠在 5 月至 10 月期间在内华达州尤其活跃,可能未被发现。内华达州法律规定,狗、猫和雪貂必须使用 狂犬病 疫苗接种,某些牲畜也必须使用 疫苗。当局强调维持目前的狂犬病并避免与野生动物直接接触,特别是蝙蝠,它们可以携带狂犬病而没有明显的疾病迹象。
查看新闻五要素
时间
世界标准时间 2026 年 23 月 23 日下午 4:28。
地点与对象
内华达州,特别是里昂县、白松县和克拉克县。
事件
自今年年初以来,三只蝙蝠检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性,内华达州农业部敦促居民为他们的宠物接种 狂犬病 疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
里昂、白松和克拉克县的三只蝙蝠检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性。狂犬病 可以影响狗、猫、牲畜和人类,被描述为一种致命病毒。
应对、状态与不确定性
内华达州法律要求主人为他们的狗、猫和雪貂接种 狂犬病 疫苗。疫苗 也可用于某些牲畜。年度监测继续识别携带 狂犬病 的蝙蝠,加强了预防性护理的必要性。测试有助于追踪病毒并向公众和动物卫生机构提供信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nevada urges residents to vaccinate pets against rabies - FOX5 Vegas

News Brief
The Nevada Department of Agriculture is urging residents to vaccinate their pets against rabies following the identification of three rabies-positive bats in Lyon, White Pine and Clark counties since the beginning of 2026. State Veterinarian Dr. emphasizes that proper vaccination can effectively control rabies, protecting both animals and humans. The deadly virus affects dogs, cats, livestock, and humans, with bats being particularly active and potentially undetected in Nevada between May and October. Nevada law mandates rabies vaccinations for dogs, cats, and ferrets, with vaccines also available for certain livestock. Authorities stress maintaining current vaccinations and avoiding direct contact with wildlife, especially bats, which can carry rabies without obvious signs of illness.
Original source text (English)

(KOLO) - The Nevada Department of Agriculture is urging residents to vaccinate their pets against rabies.They say that since the start of the year, their Animal Disease Laboratory has identified three bats in Lyon, White Pine and Clark counties that have tested positive for rabies.“Rabies can be effectively controlled with proper vaccination,” said NDA State Veterinarian Dr. Peter Rolfe, DVM. “Keeping pets up to date on vaccines is the best way to protect both animals and the people around them.”The deadly virus can affect dogs, cats, livestock and humans. Bats are active in the state of Nevada between May and October and can enter homes or barns undetected.“Annual monitoring in Nevada continues to identify bats that test positive for rabies, reinforcing the need for preventive care,” added Laura Morrow, NDA Animal Disease Laboratory supervisor. “Testing helps us track where the virus is present and provide information to the public and animal health agencies, supporting their response to potential exposures.” Nevada law requires owners to vaccinate their dogs, cats and ferrets to be vaccinated against rabies. Vaccines are also available for certain kinds of livestock.“Maintaining current rabies vaccinations for pets and avoiding direct contact with wildlife are critical steps in reducing risk,” said NDA Director J.J. Goicoechea, DVM. “Bats, in particular, should never be handled, as they can carry rabies even when no obvious signs of illness are present.”Copyright 2026 KOLO. 野生动物园餐厅附近发现狂犬病蝙蝠 发出健康警告 - 新唐人

Rabid Bat Found Near Safari Park Restaurant Prompts Health Warning - NTD News
新闻简报7 月 11 日,圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园一家餐厅外发现一只死蝙蝠,其 狂犬病 检测呈阳性,促使县卫生官员寻找任何可能接触过该动物的人。这只蝙蝠是在中午 12 点 10 分发现的,并不属于公园的收藏品。一名经过培训的员工收集了该病毒,并于 7 月 13 日进行了测试,确认为 狂犬病。目前尚未报告人与人接触的情况,但官员们敦促任何有直接接触的人立即致电 (619) 692-8499。这是圣地亚哥县今年发现的第 19 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠。卫生官员强调,如果不及早治疗,狂犬病 几乎 100% 致命,并且正在调查以确保接触者得到及时护理。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-22。事件日期:2026年7月11日,中午12:10。
地点与对象
圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园,岩石绿洲餐厅,圣地亚哥县,加利福尼亚州。
事件
在野生动物园餐厅发现的一只死蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测结果呈阳性。县卫生官员正在寻找任何可能与该动物有直接接触的人。这只蝙蝠不属于公园的动物收藏。
规模、影响与风险
这是 2026 年在圣地亚哥县发现的第 19 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠。迄今为止尚未有人类接触的报道。只有直接身体接触的人才面临感染风险。如果不及早发现,狂犬病 几乎 100% 致命。
应对、状态与不确定性
一名训练有素的公园工作人员收集了蝙蝠。7 月 13 日在县公共卫生实验室进行了检测,确认了 狂犬病。县公共卫生服务中心要求任何有直接联系的人立即致电 (619) 692-8499。官员们正在进行调查,以确保任何接触过病毒的人都能迅速得到护理。标准预防措施包括避免接触野生动物并在接触后寻求医疗建议。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabid Bat Found Near Safari Park Restaurant Prompts Health Warning - NTD News

News Brief
A dead bat found outside a restaurant at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park on July 11 has tested positive for rabies, prompting county health officials to seek anyone who may have touched the animal. The bat, found at 12:10 p.m., was not part of the park's collection. A trained employee collected it, and testing confirmed rabies on July 13. No human contact has been reported, but officials are urging anyone with direct contact to call (619) 692-8499 immediately. This marks the 19th rabid bat detected in San Diego County this year. Health officials stress that rabies is nearly 100% fatal if not treated early and are investigating to ensure exposed individuals receive prompt care.
Original source text (English)
This is an undated closeup photo of the eastern pipistrelle bat, a species that is frequently linked with human rabies cases. (Merlin D./The Canadian Press/AP) A bat found dead outside a restaurant at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park has tested positive for rabies, prompting county health officials to seek out anyone who may have touched the animal. The bat was found at 12:10 p.m. on July 11 at the Oasis on the Rocks restaurant inside the Safari Park, according to the county. A trained park employee collected the animal, and officials said no human contact with the bat has been reported so far, according to a recent San Diego County News Center press release . County officials stressed that the bat was not part of the park's animal collection. The specimen was taken to the County Public Health Laboratory on July 13, where testing confirmed rabies. Health Officials Seek Close Contacts County Public Health Services is asking anyone who had direct contact with the bat—including touching or holding it—or who knows someone who may have, to call (619) 692-8499 immediately. Officials noted that only people with direct physical contact face any risk of infection. "Rabies is an extremely serious viral disease, one of the deadliest, with nearly 100% fatality if not caught early enough," said Dr. Ankita Kadakia, the county's deputy public health officer. "We are investigating to ensure anyone who came into contact with the bat receives care quickly. Once symptoms start there is no treatment for rabies," Kadakia said. Latest In A String Of Cases This marks the 19th bat to test positive for rabies in San Diego County so far this year. That figure trails the 21 rabid bats recorded in all of 2025. The Safari Park has seen rabid bats before. A separate county release from March 25 detailed two earlier cases: a bat found March 21 near the White Oak Villas condo complex in Escondido and another found March 22 at the park's Okavango Restaurant, both of which later tested positive. Those two cases were the fourth and fifth rabid bats detected in the county this year. Dr. Sayone Thihalilopavan, the county's public health officer, said earlier this year that rabies can spread through a bite or when an infected bat's saliva contacts a cut, scrape, or the eyes, nose, or mouth. "Human rabies is almost always fatal without prompt post-exposure vaccination and treatment," Thihalilopavan said. County health officials reiterated standard precautions: avoid touching bats or any wildlife, and wash any exposed skin with soap and seek medical advice immediately after contact with a bat. Rabies symptoms can take weeks or months to surface, but the disease is almost always fatal once symptoms begin. Human rabies cases remain rare. California's most recent human case was recorded in 2024, when a Fresno County resident was exposed to a bat in Merced County. Officials noted that bats serve an important ecological role in San Diego County, feeding on insects like mosquitoes and pollinating plants through nectar feeding. Residents may spot bats around sunset, when the animals are most active, and officials urged the public to give wildlife space. Conversation 2 Comments Conversations are moderated. For more info, see our Community Guidelines

Scale, impact and risk

Response, status and uncertainty

Standard precautions include avoiding touching wildlife and seeking medical advice after contact.

马来巴来市加大打击流浪狗力度 - Inquirer.net

Malaybalay City ramps up crackdown vs stray dogs - Inquirer.net
新闻简报马来巴来市已加强全市范围的"收容犬只"运动,以控制狂犬病病例、咬伤事件和交通事故。根据共和国法案9482,该行动已导致33只狗被安乐死,44只被收容,10只被领回,1只被收养。当地当局报告称公共交通事故和咬伤事件增加,给医疗和财政资源带来压力。捕获的动物在城市兽医设施接受护理,主人需支付P1,100的罚款,并在三天内领回动物。未被领养的动物可供收养或接受人道安乐死。市长杰·沃伦·帕比拉兰强调执行《反狂犬病法》对保护公民(尤其是儿童)的重要性,同时敦促宠物主人将宠物圈养并参与免费的疫苗接种项目。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-25;最近,所有描笼涯的事件愈演愈烈。
地点与对象
菲律宾布基农马来巴莱市;受影响的人群包括公民,尤其是儿童和宠物主人。
事件
根据第 9482 号共和国法案(2007 年反 狂犬病 法案),确认全市范围内针对流浪狗的“Dakip-Aso”运动,以遏制 狂犬病 案件、咬伤事件和街头事故。
规模、影响与风险
33只狗被安乐死,44只被扣押,10只被回收,1只被收养;公共道路事故和咬伤事件增加,导致对医疗和财政援助的需求增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
配备网和运输车辆的团队正在巡逻;捕获的动物获得庇护所、食物、疫苗接种和基本护理;宠物主人需支付P1,100的罚款,并在三天内领回动物;未被领养的动物可供收养或接受人道安乐死;政府提供免费的疫苗接种项目。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Malaybalay City ramps up crackdown vs stray dogs - Inquirer.net

News Brief
Malaybalay City has intensified its citywide 'Dakip-Aso' campaign against stray dogs to curb rabies cases, bite incidents, and road accidents. Under Republic Act 9482, the initiative has resulted in 33 dogs euthanized, 44 impounded, 10 reclaimed, and 1 adopted. Local authorities report increased public road accidents and bite incidents, straining medical and financial resources. Captured animals receive care at the city veterinary facility, with owners given three days to reclaim them after a P1,100 penalty. Unclaimed animals are offered for adoption or undergo humane euthanasia. Mayor Jay Warren Pabillaran emphasized the importance of enforcing the Anti-Rabies Act to protect citizens, especially children, while urging pet owners to keep pets confined and participate in free vaccination programs.
Original source text (English)
News Opinion Sports Lifestyle POP Preen Entertainment Business Technology Global Nation USA & Canada Cebu Daily News Bandera Libre Esports MotionCars Radio INQUIRER Follow Us: Close the sidebar Malaybalay City ramps up crackdown vs stray dogs By: Mel N. Velez - 5 hours ago Personnel from the Malaybalay City Veterinary Office prepare a large net to catch a stray dog during a “Dakip-Aso” operation. The city government has intensified its stray-catching drive across all barangays under Republic Act 9482 (Anti-Rabies Act of 2007) to curb rabies cases, bite incidents, and street accidents. (Photo by LGU Malaybalay City) MALAYBALAY CITY, Bukidnon — The city government recently intensified its citywide “Dakip-Aso” campaign against stray dogs across all barangays, resulting in 33 dogs euthanized, 44 impounded, 10 reclaimed by their owners and one adopted. The initiative strictly enforces Republic Act No. 9482, or the Anti-Rabies Act of 2007. Local authorities aim to eliminate human rabies fatalities, reduce animal bite incidents, and minimize road accidents caused by unconfined pets. Teams equipped with nets and transport vehicles are patrolling local thoroughfares to impound roaming animals. City veterinarian Jude Ignatius Casimero stated that free-roaming animals have increased public road accidents and bite incidents, resulting in greater demand on the local government for medical and financial assistance. Captured animals are taken to the city veterinary facility where they receive temporary shelter, food, vaccinations and basic care. Under city guidelines, pet owners have three days to retrieve impounded animals after paying a P1,100 penalty. Unclaimed animals are offered for adoption. The unadopted ones undergo humane euthanasia as a final resort. Malaybalay City Mayor Jay Warren Pabillaran reminded owners to keep pets leashed or confined to private property and encouraged participation in free government vaccination programs. “Enforcing the Anti-Rabies Act is not just about impounding stray animals—it is a vital measure to protect our citizens, especially children, from rabies while ensuring pet owners take full responsibility for their animals,” Pabillaran said. 皮马县 7 月份接触蝙蝠数量创历史新高:如果遇到蝙蝠该怎么办 - KGUN 9

Pima County sees record bat exposures in July: what to do if you encounter one - KGUN 9
新闻简报2026 年 7 月,亚利桑那州皮马县接触蝙蝠的数量创下历史新高,卫生官员报告称,蝙蝠接触数量大幅增加,超过了前几年的峰值。仅上周,图森医疗中心就治疗了约 20 名患者,并使用 狂犬病 疫苗 作为预防措施。今年到目前为止,皮马县卫生部门已记录了 194 起蝙蝠与人接触的事件,7 月份的数量已超过 2025 年的峰值。卫生官员敦促公众不要触摸蝙蝠,无论是死的还是活的,如果蝙蝠进入他们的家,请致电皮马动物护理中心。对蝙蝠进行 狂犬病 检测,结果决定是否需要医疗护理。狂犬病病毒 在环境中很脆弱,但需要进行身体接触或咬伤评估。建议居民从远处观察蝙蝠并报告异常行为,例如白天活动或表现出生病的样子。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 24 日。事件发生于 2026 年 7 月,上周和过去三周出现峰值。
地点与对象
美国亚利桑那州皮马县。受影响人群包括遭遇蝙蝠的居民,具体地点包括:潘塔诺沃什河上的东百老汇大桥、里利托河上的北坎贝尔大道大桥、里利托河上的东坦克维德大桥和圣克鲁斯河上的伊娜路大桥。
事件
2026 年 7 月,亚利桑那州皮马县蝙蝠暴露数量创历史新高。上周,图森医疗中心治疗了约 20 名蝙蝠暴露患者,皮马县卫生局报告称,2026 年迄今已有 194 例蝙蝠暴露患者,超过了 2025 年的峰值。卫生官员证实了这一增加,并提供了处理蝙蝠遭遇的指导。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年到目前为止,皮马县报告了 194 起蝙蝠接触事件,其中 7 月份的数量已经超过了去年的峰值。图森医疗中心上周收治了大约 20 名患者。风险包括潜在的 狂犬病 传播,但由于蝙蝠体型较小,咬伤可能并不总是可见。卫生官员根据暴露细节将情况分为低风险、中风险或高风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员正在调查并应对蝙蝠接触量激增的情况。图森医疗中心使用 狂犬病 疫苗 和免疫球蛋白作为预防措施。皮马县卫生部门评估每个病例以确定风险级别,并建议对中度或高风险暴露进行医疗护理。对蝙蝠进行 狂犬病 测试,结果指导进一步的行动。建议公众切勿触摸蝙蝠,如果家中出现蝙蝠,请致电皮马动物护理中心,如果接触蝙蝠,请就医。关于蝙蝠活动增加的确切原因仍存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pima County sees record bat exposures in July: what to do if you encounter one - KGUN 9

News Brief
Pima County, Arizona, is experiencing a record number of bat exposures in July 2026, with health officials reporting a significant spike surpassing previous years' peaks. Tucson Medical Center has treated about 20 patients in the last week alone, administering rabies vaccines as a precaution. The Pima County Health Department has documented 194 bat exposures to people so far this year, with July's numbers already exceeding the 2025 peak. Health officials urge the public to never touch bats, whether dead or alive, and to call the Pima Animal Care Center if a bat enters their home. Bats are tested for rabies, and results determine if medical care is needed. The rabies virus is fragile in the environment, but physical contact or bites require assessment. Residents are advised to observe bats from a distance and report unusual behavior, such as daytime activity or appearing sick.
Original source text (English)

Health officials say never touch a bat and call PACC if one enters your home. Prev Next Pima County bat exposures have hit a record high. Here's what health officials say you should do if you encounter one. By: Vanessa Gongora Posted 5:25 AM, Jul 24, 2026 and last updated 6:07 PM, Jul 24, 2026 TUCSON, Ariz. — It’s the time of year when bats are seen throughout Pima County, and it is a fascinating experience where many people go to nearby bridges and wait for them to come out at sunset. You always want to make sure you keep your distance.Tucson Medical Center's (TMC) emergency department has seen a significant spike in patients seeking treatment for bat exposure, and Pima County health officials say the number of bat-related animal exposures has already surpassed 2025's peak on record. 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGETMC's Pediatric Emergency Department Manager Hannah Gerbracht said the hospital has treated about 20 patients in the last week alone, administering rabies vaccines as a precaution."We obviously do an assessment. Ask what the bats looked like, were they awake, were they acting weird, and then we do prophylactically treat everybody who comes into the ER with rabies vaccines and immunoglobulin," Gerbracht said.Gerbracht said bat exposures don't always mean you were necessarily bitten, but you were close in proximity. "Whether that means bats in their house or in a pool that they've been in, but we've seen a great deal over the last probably three weeks, coming in for bats in their houses," Gerbracht said.The Pima County Health Department (PCHD) has dealt with194 bats exposed to people so far this year. Dr. Ellen Santos, PCHD's Epidemiology Program Manager, said July's numbers are already skyrocketing. PCHD "So this black dotted line here, this is the number of animal exposures in people that we dealt with last year, so in 2025. And so we peaked in August, but you can see for July, and the month's not even over yet, we are above any peak that we've seen," Dr. Santos said.Health officials urge the public never to touch a bat, whether it is dead or alive. If a pet has a bat in its mouth, Santos said, stay protected when trying to remove it. "If your dog brings in a bat from outside, you know, try to get it away from your dog with like, you know, a plastic, some sort of barrier or gloves or something," Dr. Santos said. "And then call your veterinarian to do an assessment for your animal," Dr. Santos said.If a bat is found inside a home, residents can call Pima Animal Care Center (PACC) to retrieve it. The bat will then be tested for rabies, and the results will determine whether the people in the home need to seek medical care."What usually happens is if someone encounters a bat or a wild animal, they will call us, or it'll get reported to us somehow, and we will reach out to that person, and we'll ask them a whole bunch of questions about what happened and all that is to help us determine, is this a low risk situation, is this a high risk situation? And for anything that is kind of moderate or high risk, we might recommend seeking healthcare," Dr. Santos said. She said there are visible signs that a bat may be infected with rabies."Some symptoms you might see if the bat does have rabies and is symptomatic, they might be kind of flying around during the day when we don't expect to see them. They might appear kind of sick," Santos said. "You might find them on the ground, kind of flopping around. You might find them kind of moving around in a way that you wouldn't expect them to be flying. So, any kind of unusual-looking behavior is usually suspicious."One patient treated at TMC found a bat in their pool. Dr. Santos said residents in that situation do not need to drain the pool."Even if that bat has rabies, the rabies virus is very fragile in the environment, so exposure to things like sun, sunlight, UV light outside of a warm-blooded animal, a mammal, that rabies virus is not going to survive," Santos said. "But if anyone encountered that bat, like physically touched it, or, you know, if the bat flew at them and they notice a bite or a scratch, that's when we would want to do an assessment, and we'd be a little more concerned."She says one thing to remember about bats is that they are really tiny, as well as their teeth, so if a bat bites you, you don't always see it or know that it's there."So that's why if you wake up at home and you notice that a bat has been flying around, that we consider a high-risk situation because while you're asleep or not aware of what's going on, we don't know if that bat came up to you or bit because those bites are so tiny," Dr. Santos said.Bats are a protected species, and there are several popular locations in the area to observe them. Lake County confirms first rabid bat in 4 years - spectrumnews1.com
新闻简报俄亥俄州莱克县确认了四年来首例患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,发现于佩恩斯维尔的一名居民家中。莱克县综合卫生区于 2026 年 7 月 24 日星期四宣布了该病例。虽然蝙蝠有利于昆虫控制,但它们可以携带 狂犬病,这种病毒通过咬伤、抓伤或唾液接触开放性伤口传播。卫生官员强调,居民不应害怕户外活动,但应保持谨慎并在附近饲养宠物。任何可能暴露的人都应立即寻求 暴露后预防 (PEP) 的医疗救助,该方法几乎 100% 有效。俄亥俄州今年迄今已报告 15 起蝙蝠 狂犬病 病例,其中 2025 年为 19 起,2024 年为 43 起。居民如果在家中发现蝙蝠,应采取安全措施。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Lake County confirms first rabid bat in 4 years - spectrumnews1.com

News Brief
Lake County, Ohio has confirmed its first rabid bat in four years, found in a resident's home in Painesville. The Lake County General Health District announced the case on Thursday, July 24, 2026. While bats are beneficial for insect control, they can carry rabies, which is transmitted through bites, scratches, or saliva contact with open wounds. Health officials emphasize that residents should not fear outdoor activities but should remain cautious and keep pets nearby. Anyone potentially exposed is urged to seek immediate medical attention for post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP), which is nearly 100% effective. Ohio has reported 15 rabies cases in bats so far this year, with 19 in 2025 and 43 in 2024. Residents are advised on safe steps to follow if they find a bat in their home.
Original source text (English)
PAINESVILLE, Ohio — The Lake County General Health District announced Thursday that a bat from Painesville tested positive for rabies. A resident reported the bat, which was found in their home. It's the first rabid bat to be confirmed rabid in the county since 2022. "Bats are beneficial, as they eat large numbers of flying insects, but bats may also be a source of rabies," said LCGHD Environmental Health Supervisor Bert Mechenbier. Mechenbier said humans and pets can get the virus if they are either bitten or scratched by an infected animal. It can also happen when saliva from an infected animal gets into an open wound. Despite this, Health Commissioner Adam Litke said people should not worry. "Residents should not fear spending time outside, but should be aware and keep their pets nearby," Litke said. Those who believe they may have been exposed to rabies should seek immediate medical attention. Individuals will go through a process called post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) which includes wound care, human rabies immune globulin and a series of four or five rabies vaccines, which must be given as soon as possible after exposure, according to the department. The process is nearly 100% effective. If Ohioans find a bat in their homes, they're encouraged to take the following steps: - Close all windows and doors, and leave the bat in the room - Call a professional to capture the bat - If there is no professional to capture the bat, Miami County Public Health recommends putting on leather gloves, slowly approaching the bat when it lands, and placing a see-through container over the bat. Then, slide a lid under the container to trap the bat. Secure the lid with tape and punch small holes in the container to allow the bat to breathe. So far this year, there have been 15 confirmed rabies cases in Ohio and all from bats — not including this recent confirmation in Lake County, according to the Ohio Department of Health. In mid-May, that number stood at seven. In 2025, there were 19 confirmed cases of bats with rabies across the state, a sharp decline from 2024, which had 43. In Ohio, there are more than 20,000 bite exposures and 1,000 non-bite exposures are reported in Ohio each year, exposing more than 21,000 people, according to ODH. Non-bite exposures can happen when a bat is found in a room with a child, someone who is sleeping or someone with a sensory or mental impairment
View five news elements
Time
Published on 2026-07-24. Event date: Thursday (not specified which Thursday, but likely the week of publication).
Location and population
Lake County, Ohio, USA. Specifically in Painesville. Affected population: residents who may have been exposed to the rabid bat.
Event
Confirmed rabid bat found in a resident's home in Painesville, Ohio. This is the first confirmed rabid bat in Lake County since 2022. Reported by the Lake County General Health District.
Scale, impact and risk
One confirmed rabid bat case. No human or animal cases mentioned. Risk: Humans and pets can be exposed through bites, scratches, or saliva contact with open wounds. Ohio has had 15 confirmed rabies cases in bats so far this year (excluding this case), with 19 cases in 2025 and 43 in 2024.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials advise residents not to fear spending time outside but to be aware and keep pets nearby. Those exposed should seek immediate medical attention for post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP), which is nearly 100% effective. Steps for handling bats in homes are provided. No ongoing investigation or unresolved information mentioned.

Health officials issue dire warning to unsuspecting restaurant guests possibly exposed to rabies - New York Post
新闻简报一只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠落在一家熟食店的一个家庭的桌子上后,圣地亚哥的卫生官员向餐厅的客人发出警告。这只蝙蝠在人道协会检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性,是该县今年第 22 例确诊病例,超过了去年的总数。虽然没有直接接触的报道,但官员们敦促任何可能接触过蝙蝠的人寻求医疗建议。一旦出现症状,狂犬病 几乎总是致命的,但可以通过提示 治疗 来预防。这一事件凸显了尊重野生动物和避免与蝙蝠接触的重要性,蝙蝠通过控制昆虫种群和为植物授粉在当地生态系统中发挥着至关重要的作用。

美国加利福尼亚州圣地亚哥县;特别是在圣地亚哥的一家熟食店;受影响的人群包括可能接触过蝙蝠的餐厅客人。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health officials issue dire warning to unsuspecting restaurant guests possibly exposed to rabies - New York Post

News Brief
Health officials in San Diego are warning restaurant guests after a rabid bat landed on a family's table at a deli. The bat, which tested positive for rabies at the Humane Society, is the 22nd confirmed case in the county this year, surpassing last year's total. While no direct contact was reported, officials are urging anyone who may have touched the bat to seek medical advice. Rabies is almost always fatal once symptoms appear but can be prevented with prompt treatment. This incident highlights the importance of respecting wildlife and avoiding contact with bats, which play a crucial role in the local ecosystem by controlling insect populations and pollinating plants.
Original source text (English)
Health officials issue dire warning to unsuspecting restaurant guests possibly exposed to rabies See more of our coverage in your search results. Add The California Post on GoogleLunch with a side of rabid bat. A rabid bat landed on a munching family’s table at a San Diego deli restaurant this past Sunday. San Diego public health officials are concerned, issuing a notice to anyone who may have touched the bat that they are at risk of contracting rabies and should call their healthcare provider. No direct contact with the bat was reported. A diner scooped up the bat with a towel and transported it to the Humane Society in Oceanside, where it tested positive for the highly fatal disease. The bat is the 22nd to test positive for rabies in San Diego County this year, already beating 2025’s 21 rabid bats. It’s the most confirmed rabies-positive bats since at least 2021, according to county data. Sign up for the California Morning Report newsletter California's top news, sports and entertainment delivered to your inbox every day. Thanks for signing up! Bats are the only species to test positive for rabies this year. San Diego officials reminded residents: - Respect the space of wildlife, do not touch or approach bats or any wildlife. - If you have direct contact with a bat, wash the exposed area with soap and seek medical advice. - Rabies symptoms can take weeks to months to appear, and once symptoms begin, the disease is almost always fatal. Prompt treatment after an exposure is highly effective. The state hasn’t had a human rabies case since 2024, when a Fresno County resident was exposed to a rabid bat in Merced County. Rabies is almost always fatal once symptoms are seen but is considered preventable with treatment. San Diego officials emphasized the importance of bats in the local ecosystem. “Bats play an important role in our local ecosystem,” they wrote. “Many species of bats live in San Diego County, feeding on insects such as mosquitos. Other bat species feed on nectar from plants and pollinate them in the process. You may see bats occasionally around sunset when they are most active. It is important to respect wildlife and give wild animals space to play their role in our natural environment. ” What do you think? Post a comment. Fewer than 10 Americans die from rabies every year, according to the CDC. It’s unclear what the bat ordered, but the family hopes the flying mammal is not giving them any leftovers to take home. 蝙蝠 狂犬病 检测呈阳性后,向毫无戒心的食客发出健康警告 - mb.ntd.com

Health Warning Issued for Unsuspecting Diners After Bat Tests Positive for Rabies - mb.ntd.com
新闻简报圣地亚哥县卫生官员在 Vista 餐厅的一只蝙蝠检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性后发出警告。7 月 19 日,一家人吃饭时,这只蝙蝠落在了桌子上。虽然没有直接接触的报道,但任何接触过这只动物的人都应立即就医。这标志着该县今年出现了第22只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,超过了去年的总数。卫生官员强调,一旦出现症状,狂犬病 几乎 100% 致命,但可以通过及时 治疗 进行预防。提醒居民避开野生动物并报告任何接触情况。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026年7月24日。蝙蝠事件发生在下午1点。2026 年 7 月 19 日,星期日。
地点与对象
圣地亚哥县,维斯塔,黄色熟食店。餐厅的食客和工作人员可能受到感染。
事件
确认 狂犬病 病例是一只落在餐厅桌子上的蝙蝠。卫生官员向任何可能接触过这只蝙蝠的人发出警告。
规模、影响与风险
没有直接皮肤接触的报道。只有直接身体接触的人才面临感染风险。这是 2026 年圣地亚哥县第 22 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,超过了去年的 21 只蝙蝠。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员敦促接触者立即就医。狂犬病 暴露后预防 在症状出现前有效,但一旦症状出现则致命。调查正在进行中,以确保识别出所有潜在的暴露情况。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health Warning Issued for Unsuspecting Diners After Bat Tests Positive for Rabies - mb.ntd.com

News Brief
San Diego County health officials have issued a warning after a bat tested positive for rabies at a Vista restaurant. The bat landed on a table during a family's meal on July 19. While no direct contact has been reported, anyone who handled the animal is urged to seek medical care immediately. This marks the 22nd rabid bat in the county this year, exceeding last year's total. Health officials emphasize rabies is nearly 100% fatal once symptoms appear but preventable with timely treatment. Residents are reminded to avoid wildlife and report any exposures.
Original source text (English)
A pallid bat flying over a pond at night in a file photo. (Shutterstock) San Diego County health officials are asking anyone who may have handled a bat that landed on a table at a Vista restaurant last weekend to seek medical attention after lab tests confirmed the animal was infected with rabies. The bat appeared at the Yellow Deli Restaurant in Vista at 1 p.m. Sunday, July 19, landing on a table while a family was dining, the County of San Diego Communications Office said Thursday. Another customer used a towel to capture the animal and brought it to the Humane Society in Oceanside, which then transferred it to the County's Public Health Lab, where testing confirmed rabies. County officials said no direct skin contact with the bat has been reported so far. Still, anyone who touched or held the animal, or who knows someone who may have, is being urged to contact a health care provider immediately, since only people with direct physical contact face any risk of infection. Health officials said that rabies post-exposure prophylaxis works well when administered before symptoms appear, but once symptoms set in, there is no treatment and the disease is almost always fatal. The Vista incident is the 22nd bat to test positive for rabies in San Diego County this year, surpassing the 21 rabid bats recorded in all of 2025. That tally builds on a case reported just days earlier, when a bat found dead outside the Oasis on the Rocks restaurant at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park tested positive for rabies after being collected by a park employee on July 11. That bat was confirmed as the 19th of the year. Two earlier rabid bats were also found in March near Escondido's White Oak Villas condo complex and at the Safari Park's Okavango Restaurant. "Rabies is an extremely serious viral disease, one of the deadliest, with nearly 100% fatality if not caught early enough," said Dr. Ankita Kadakia, the county's deputy public health officer, in an announcement following the earlier incident. "We are investigating to ensure anyone who came into contact with the bat receives care quickly. Once symptoms start there is no treatment for rabies.” County Public Health Services continues to urge residents to avoid touching or approaching bats or any wildlife, and to wash any exposed skin with soap and seek medical advice immediately after contact. Rabies symptoms can take weeks to months to appear, but the illness is almost always fatal once symptoms begin. Human rabies cases remain rare. California's most recent human case occurred in 2024, involving a Fresno County resident exposed to a bat in Merced County. Despite the recent cases, officials noted bats play an important ecological role in San Diego County, feeding on insects such as mosquitoes and pollinating plants by feeding on nectar. Residents may spot bats around sunset, when they are most active, and officials asked the public to give the animals space. Anyone with questions about a possible exposure or rabies prevention can contact the county's Epidemiology unit at (619) 692-8499. Conversation Conversations are moderated. For more info, see our Community Guidelines
View five news elements
Time
Published on July 24, 2026. The bat incident occurred at 1 p.m. on Sunday, July 19, 2026.
Location and population
San Diego County, Vista, Yellow Deli Restaurant. Diners and staff at the restaurant were potentially exposed.
Event
Confirmed rabies case in a bat that landed on a table at a restaurant. Health officials issued a warning for anyone who may have handled the bat.
Scale, impact and risk
No direct skin contact reported. Only people with direct physical contact face risk of infection. This is the 22nd rabid bat in San Diego County in 2026, surpassing last year's total of 21.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials are urging exposed individuals to seek medical attention immediately. Rabies post-exposure prophylaxis is effective before symptoms appear but fatal once symptoms start. Investigation is ongoing to ensure all potential exposures are identified.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

WNC 野生动物康复者在浣熊安乐死后对法律提出异议 - 阿什维尔公民时报

WNC wildlife rehabilitator disputes laws after raccoons euthanized - The Asheville Citizen Times
新闻简报在北卡罗来纳州西阿什维尔,有执照的野生动物康复者米歇尔·科尔在州官员扣押并安乐死五只浣熊后面临指控。Cole 是 Alleyoop 野生动物救援与康复组织的运营商,去年秋天救出了小浣熊,但缺乏所需的 狂犬病 媒介 物种许可证。尽管对动物进行了疫苗接种,但州法律禁止在未经认证的情况下饲养此类动物超过 24 小时。根据 狂犬病 预防指南,浣熊被安乐死,这促使科尔质疑这些法律是否残酷。卫生官员在过去六个月中建议设施访客寻求 狂犬病 风险评估,尽管这被认为是预防性的。科尔拥有预先曝光的 狂犬病 镜头,他认为这些动物是安全的,并质疑法律的道德性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 23 日。该事件发生在 2025 年底,当时浣熊被抓获,并于 2026 年 7 月 13 日提出指控。
地点与对象
北卡罗来纳州西阿什维尔,特别是沙山路的 Alleyoop 野生动物救援和康复中心。受影响的人群包括过去六个月内访问过该设施的访客,建议他们进行 狂犬病 风险评估。
事件
北卡罗来纳州野生动物资源委员会扣押并安乐死了五只浣熊,一名持有执照的野生动物康复师米歇尔·科尔 (Michelle Cole) 被指控未经许可持有 狂犬病 媒介 物种。科尔对法律提出异议,声称她遵循正确的 疫苗接种 协议,并且不了解具体的县政策。
规模、影响与风险
班库姆县卫生与公众服务部向该设施的游客发出警告,要求其获得 狂犬病 风险评估,不过野生动物资源委员会称这是一项预防措施。尚未报告人类 狂犬病 病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
对科尔设施的调查正在进行中。野生动物官员在癫痫发作期间遵循了协议,并根据 狂犬病 预防计划指南进行了安乐死。科尔坚称浣熊已接种疫苗,不会构成 狂犬病 风险,但州法律禁止在没有适当认证的情况下饲养 狂犬病 媒介 物种超过 24 小时。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

WNC wildlife rehabilitator disputes laws after raccoons euthanized - The Asheville Citizen Times

News Brief
In West Asheville, North Carolina, licensed wildlife rehabilitator Michelle Cole faces charges after five raccoons were seized and euthanized by state officials. Cole, operator of Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation, rescued the baby raccoons last fall but lacked the required rabies vector species permit. Despite vaccinating the animals, state law prohibits holding such species longer than 24 hours without certification. The raccoons were euthanized per rabies prevention guidelines, prompting Cole to dispute the laws as cruel. Health officials advised facility visitors in the last six months to seek rabies risk assessments, though this was deemed precautionary. Cole, who has pre-exposure rabies shots, argues the animals were safe and questions the morality of the laws.
Original source text (English)
Local Sports High School Huddle Scene Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals LOCALWNC wildlife rehabilitator disputes laws after raccoons euthanized Kara FohnerAsheville Citizen TimesUpdated July 23, 2026, 9:57 a.m. ETA wildlife rehabilitator in Asheville was charged after five raccoons were seized from her facility.The operator did not have the required state permit to possess rabies vector species like raccoons.Health officials advised anyone who visited the facility in the last six months to get a rabies risk assessment.ASHEVILLE - A licensed wildlife rehabilitation operator in West Asheville found herself at odds with North Carolina wildlife laws after she rescued baby raccoons, which were later seized and euthanized by the North Carolina Wildlife Resources Commission.Michelle Cole, operator of Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation on Sand Hill Road, told the Citizen Times July 17 that she obtained the five raccoons that were seized last fall, when they were babies."They came to me when they were four weeks old, eyes still closed, four of them did. The other one was about five weeks old, and it had had its arm chopped off with a chainsaw," she said. "So, I bottle fed them, raised them. ... They've all had their rabies shots and their distemper shots."In the State of North Carolina, licensed wildlife rehabilitators must have a captivity license for rehabilitation, along with an additional rabies vector species certification, Buncombe County said in a July 16 news release. They also must have a complete rabies pre-exposure series of vaccines to be allowed to handle and rehabilitate animal species that are at high risk of carrying rabies virus, which includes bats, raccoons, skunks, foxes, and bobcats. In Buncombe County and other counties where the US Department of Agriculture’s Oral Rabies Vaccination program is conducted, the rehabilitation and release of rabies vector species is not authorized, except for bats.Cole said that she initially reached out to the N.C. Wildlife Resources Commission and asked how rabies vector species should be handled, although she didn't tell them she had rescued raccoons. She said she didn't at first know about Buncombe County's policy, but she said she was told by state officials that she was "supposed to walk away and let them die.""I'm not going to kill them. I already had them. I was really looking for what to do with them, if I could take them somewhere," she said. Cole feels the policy in Buncombe County is cruel, and so she kept the raccoons, with plans to release them into the wild when they were fully rehabilitated. Recently, she said someone she thought was a friend came over and saw one of the raccoons. That person reported her to wildlife officials."It's ludicrous, really. No one knew about them. ... No one ever went back there except me, and I've had my pre-exposure shots," she said.Cole was charged July 13 with possession of a rabies vector species without a permit, the N.C. Wildlife Resources Commission told the Citizen Times July 16.The raccoons were seized and euthanized, according to the Wildlife Resources Commission."Five raccoons (an RVS) were found being held unlawfully in captivity. The raccoons were captured, secured, and placed into carriers by agency staff with Ms. Cole’s cooperation and assistance," the organization said in a statement July 22. "The animals were euthanized following RVS/ORV rabies prevention program guidance. The consequences to wildlife are extremely difficult for everyone when laws are not followed."Wildlife law enforcement leadership reviewed body camera footage of enforcement actions taken by the officer during the seizure of the raccoons and confirmed that protocol was properly followed, the Wildlife Resources Commission added.Cole feels the laws around rabies vector species are unfair."Me and my fellow colleagues have tried to make changes with Raleigh regarding this law. They don't care. They don't listen. How do you fight the state on a moral issue? If I had money, it'd be one thing. All of my money goes into saving these animals," she said.She said that the raccoons that were seized had been vaccinated against rabies."They had their shots. If the reasoning is that they were euthanized because of the possibility of rabies, they were contained for a year, they obviously didn't have rabies," she said. "They were given their shots, and they were never introduced to the public. No one ever touched them but me."The N.C. Wildlife Resources Commission said that the investigation into Cole's facility is ongoing. In a statement, N.C. Wildlife said that while Cole has a valid rehabilitator license, she was not permitted to hold rabies vector species longer than 24 hours. The Buncombe County Department of Health and Human Services warned the public in a press release July 16 that anyone who visited the facility in the last six months should obtain a rabies risk assessment. The North Carolina Wildlife Resources Commission said that the press release was a precautionary measure.Kara Fohner is the Public Safety Reporter for the Asheville Citizen Times. Time
Published on July 23, 2026. The event occurred in late 2025 when the raccoons were seized, with charges filed on July 13, 2026.
Location and population
West Asheville, North Carolina, specifically at Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation on Sand Hill Road. The affected population includes visitors to the facility in the last six months, who were advised to seek a rabies risk assessment.
Event
A licensed wildlife rehabilitator, Michelle Cole, was charged with possession of rabies vector species without a permit after five raccoons were seized and euthanized by the North Carolina Wildlife Resources Commission. Cole disputes the laws, claiming she followed proper vaccination protocols and was unaware of specific county policies.
Scale, impact and risk
The Buncombe County Department of Health and Human Services issued a warning for visitors to the facility to obtain a rabies risk assessment, though the Wildlife Resources Commission called this a precautionary measure. No human rabies cases were reported.
Response, status and uncertainty
The investigation into Cole's facility is ongoing. Wildlife officials followed protocol during the seizure, and the euthanasia was conducted per rabies prevention program guidelines. Cole maintains the raccoons were vaccinated and posed no rabies risk, but state law prohibits holding rabies vector species longer than 24 hours without proper certification.

患有狂犬病的浣熊引发萨拉索塔县健康咨询 - 萨拉索塔先驱论坛报

Rabid raccoon prompts Sarasota County health advisory - Sarasota Herald-Tribune
新闻简报萨拉索塔县卫生官员在该地区确认一只患狂犬病的浣熊后发出警告。这只浣熊于7月21日在格罗夫角大道和普罗克特路的拐角处被杀死。佛罗里达州萨拉索塔县卫生部 (DOH-萨拉索塔) 敦促居民和游客避免接触野生动物,因为 狂犬病 构成重大风险。虽然尚未报告人类病例,但该部门强调,如果被咬或抓伤,接触后 治疗 的重要性。预防措施包括给宠物接种疫苗、保护牲畜以及防止野生动物进入生活空间。建议公众联系萨拉索塔卫生部或动物服务部门寻求帮助或报告。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 23 日;事件发生于 2026 年 7 月 21 日星期二。
地点与对象
佛罗里达州萨拉索塔县;居民和游客。
事件
佛罗里达州萨拉索塔县卫生部 (DOH-萨拉索塔) 在报告一例浣熊 狂犬病 确诊病例后发布了健康咨询。
规模、影响与风险
浣熊身上出现单例 狂犬病 确诊病例;没有人间病例报告;强调了来自野生动物的 狂犬病 暴露风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
萨拉索塔卫生部正在监测此案;建议公众避免接触野生动物,如果接触请就医;提供用于报告和协助的联系信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabid raccoon prompts Sarasota County health advisory - Sarasota Herald-Tribune

News Brief
Sarasota County health officials have issued a warning after a rabid raccoon was confirmed in the area. The raccoon was killed on July 21 at the corner of Grove Point Boulevard and Proctor Road. The Florida Department of Health in Sarasota County (DOH-Sarasota) is urging residents and visitors to avoid contact with wild animals, as rabies poses a significant risk. While no human cases have been reported, the department emphasizes the importance of post-exposure treatment if bitten or scratched. Precautions include vaccinating pets, securing livestock, and preventing wildlife from entering living spaces. Public is advised to contact DOH-Sarasota or animal services for assistance or reporting.
Original source text (English)
News Sports Ticket Lifestyle Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals SARASOTARabid raccoon prompts Sarasota County health advisoryHealth officials urge Sarasota County residents and visitors to avoid wild animals after a confirmed rabies case. Michaela GalliganSarasota Herald-TribuneJuly 23, 2026, 11:26 a.m. ETThe Florida Department of Health in Sarasota County (DOH-Sarasota) is warning residents and visitors to be aware of rabies among wild animals in the area.A press release says the health department is monitoring a single confirmed case of rabies in a raccoon. The animal was killed on the corner of Grove Point Boulevard and Proctor Road on Tuesday, July 21. DOH says that people and domestic animals should always avoid contact with wild animals such as raccoons, bats, foxes, skunks, otters, bobcats and coyotes, as they carry a higher risk of human rabies exposure and a need for post-exposure treatment. The department says that if a person is exposed to rabies, getting the appropriate treatment after exposure will protect them from the risk of rabies.DOH-Sarasota recommends taking the following precautions to prevent rabies exposure:Immunize pets and livestock based on a veterinarian’s recommended schedule.Keep pets under direct supervision and on a leash, and keep livestock secured on your property. If an animal bites your pet or livestock, seek veterinary assistance for the animal immediately and contact Sarasota Animal Services at 941-861-9501.Avoid contact with wild or stray animals. Do not handle, feed or unintentionally attract them with outdoor pet food, open garbage cans or other sources of food. If you have been bitten or scratched by a wild or domestic animal, seek medical attention, and report the injury to DOH-Sarasota by calling 941-861-2873.Never adopt wild animals or bring them into your home; instead, contact a licensed wildlife rehabilitator.Call your local animal control agency to remove any stray animals from your neighborhood. You can contact Sarasota Animal Services at 941-861-9501.Prevent wildlife, including bats, from entering living quarters or occupied spaces in homes, schools and other similar areas where they might come in contact with people and pets.DOH provides additional information on rabies at FloridaHealth.gov/Rabies. You can also contact DOH-Sarasota at 941-861-2873.Michaela Galligan is an intern covering a variety of topics for the Sarasota Herald-Tribune. She can be reached at MGalligan@usatodayco.com. 佩恩斯维尔确认患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,这是自 2022 年以来莱克县首例蝙蝠感染病例 - AOL.com

Rabid bat confirmed in Painesville, first in Lake County since 2022 - AOL.com
新闻简报据莱克县综合卫生区称,在佩恩斯维尔一处住宅中发现的一只蝙蝠已被确认患有狂犬病,这是莱克县自 2022 年以来首例此类病例。虽然蝙蝠有利于昆虫控制,但它们可以携带 狂犬病,这种病毒一旦出现症状几乎总是致命的。建议居民捕捉家中发现的任何蝙蝠,并联系卫生区进行检测。目前尚未有人类或动物感染的报告,但卫生区强调宠物 疫苗接种 和对蝙蝠行为的认识的重要性。狂犬病 在人类中仍然很少见,但却是一个严重的公共卫生问题。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 23 日;活动日期未指定,但在同一天宣布。
地点与对象
佩恩斯维尔,莱克县,俄亥俄州,美国;居民和宠物可能暴露。
事件

规模、影响与风险
没有人类或动物病例报告;狂犬病 被描述为罕见但极其致命(一旦出现症状,几乎 100% 致命);美国大多数致命的 狂犬病 病例都是蝙蝠造成的

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabid bat confirmed in Painesville, first in Lake County since 2022 - AOL.com

News Brief
A bat found in a Painesville home has been confirmed as rabid, the first such case in Lake County since 2022, according to the Lake County General Health District. While bats are beneficial for insect control, they can carry rabies, a virus that is nearly always fatal once symptoms appear. Residents are advised to capture any bats found in their homes and contact the health district for testing. No human or animal infections have been reported, but the health district emphasizes the importance of pet vaccinations and awareness of bat behavior. Rabies remains rare in humans but is a serious public health concern.
Original source text (English)
MENTOR , Ohio (WJW) — A bat recently found in a Painesville home was confirmed to be infected with the rabies virus, Lake County General Health District announced on Thursday, July 23. It’s the first confirmed case of a rabid bat in Lake County since 2022, according to a news release. “Bats are beneficial, as they eat large numbers of flying insects; but bats may also be a source of rabies,” health district Supervisor Bert Mechenbier is quoted in the release. New measles cases, possible exposures in Ashtabula County Humans and pets can become infected when bitten or scratched by an infected animal or — less commonly — when saliva from an infected animal enters an open wound or a mucous membrane like the eye or inner cheek. Residents who are exposed to bats or wake up to find a bat in the house should carefully capture the bat then call the health district at 440-350-2543 to determine if the bat should be tested for rabies, officials said. “Residents should not fear spending time outside, but should be aware and keep their pets nearby,” Health Commissioner Adam Litke is quoted in the release. What is rabies? Rabies is a rare but extremely deadly virus — “virtually 100% fatal” once symptoms appear, according to the World Health Organization . Carriers are typically bats, raccoons, skunks and foxes. An estimated 3 in 4 Americans live in a community where rabid wildlife have been found, according to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention . Though human deaths from rabies are rare — fewer than 10 each year , according to the CDC — bats are responsible for most of the fatal cases in the U.S. Each year, about 100,000 Americans are vaccinated against rabies following an exposure, according to the release. Rabies vaccinations are mandatory for domestic dogs, cats and ferrets that are at least 4 months old — even for indoor pets. A rabid bat may be able to get into a house and infect indoor pets. What are the symptoms of rabies? After exposure, the rabies virus must travel to the brain. This incubation period can last weeks or months. Without vaccination, the virus is nearly always fatal, once symptoms appear. The first symptoms may include: Flu-like illness including weakness, discomfort, fever or headache Discomfort, prickling or an itching sensation where bitten Typically, the disease takes hold two weeks after the initial symptoms. The more well-known symptoms of rabies include: Thirst and hydrophobia — the fear of water Aggressive behavior like thrashing or biting High bond set for suspect in fatal shooting at Put-in-Bay bar early Thursday How to tell if a bat has rabies Not all bats carry rabies, according to the CDC . But the ones that do usually exhibit unusual behaviors: They’re found in the daytime (most bats are nocturnal). They’re found in unusual places like inside a home or on the ground. They can’t fly or it’s easy to approach them. They make contact with humans, whom they typically avoid. Steer clear of any bats acting strangely, since that may be a sign of rabies, the CDC said. Rabid Bat Startles Patrons At San Diego County Restaurant - Patch

Rabid Bat Startles Patrons At San Diego County Restaurant - Patch

News Brief
A rabid bat landed on a table at Yellow Deli Restaurant in Vista, California, on Sunday around 1 p.m., startling patrons. The bat was collected by another diner and tested positive for rabies at the County's Public Health Lab. No direct skin contact has been reported, but officials are urging anyone who touched or held the bat to seek medical advice immediately. This marks the 22nd rabid bat in San Diego County this year. While human rabies cases remain rare in California, the disease is almost always fatal once symptoms appear, though post-exposure treatment is highly effective if administered promptly. Health officials reminded residents not to approach wildlife and to seek medical attention if exposed.
Original source text (English)
Community Corner Rabid Bat Startles Patrons At San Diego County Restaurant The bat is the 22nd in San Diego County to test positive for rabies this year. | Updated VISTA, CA — San Diego County public health officials are looking for anyone who may have had contact with a rabid bat found Sunday at a Vista restaurant. The bat landed on a table at Yellow Deli Restaurant while a family was eating around 1 p.m., according to the county. Another diner used a towel to collect the bat and brought it to the San Diego Humane Society in Oceanside, which transferred it to the County's Public Health Lab, where it tested positive for rabies. No direct skin contact with the bat has been reported, officials said. Only people who touched or held the bat are considered at risk for rabies exposure. Officials urged anyone who had direct contact with the bat or knows someone who may have to contact a health care provider as soon as possible. Rabies post-exposure treatment is highly effective when given before symptoms begin, but the disease is almost always fatal once symptoms appear. The bat is the 22nd in San Diego County to test positive for rabies this year. In 2025, 21 bats tested positive. Health officials reminded residents not to touch or approach bats or other wildlife. Anyone who comes into contact with a bat should wash the exposed area with soap and seek medical advice. Bats are a common source of rabies in California, though human cases remain rare. The state's most recent human rabies case occurred in 2024 in a Fresno County resident who was exposed to a bat in Merced County, officials said. 凯恩县官员表示,去年给宠物注射的 狂犬病 疫苗可能已经失效,已被召回 - 哥伦比亚广播公司新闻

Kane County officials say rabies shots given to pets last year may have lost potency, have been recalled - CBS News
新闻简报伊利诺伊州凯恩县官员召回了去年给宠物服用的一些 狂犬病 疫苗,因为它们可能会失去效力。疫苗 由 Elanco 制造并在 2025 年 8 月至 10 月期间提供,可能无法提供伊利诺伊州法律要求的完整 12 个月的保护。虽然该县强调受影响的宠物没有 狂犬病,但他们正在提供免费的替代疫苗,并已通过邮件联系宠物主人。宠物主人可以通过与凯恩县动物控制中心预约来安排重新接种疫苗。这种情况凸显了为宠物维护最新的 狂犬病 疫苗接种 的重要性。

规模、影响与风险
测试发现 疫苗 可能无法按预期提供完整 12 个月的保护。Elanco 免费提供了 400 次替代注射。伊利诺伊州法律要求宠物拥有当前的 狂犬病 疫苗接种。
应对、状态与不确定性
凯恩县动物控制中心向宠物主人邮寄了信件,并仅通过预约提供自愿重新接种疫苗。一些受影响的 疫苗 最早将于下个月到期。来源是完整的原始报告。

Kane County officials say rabies shots given to pets last year may have lost potency, have been recalled - CBS News

News Brief
Kane County, Illinois officials have recalled some rabies vaccines administered to pets last year due to potential loss of potency. The vaccines, manufactured by Elanco and given between August and October 2025, may not provide the full 12 months of protection as required by Illinois law. While the county stressed that affected pets do not have rabies, they are offering free replacement shots and have contacted pet owners via mail. Pet owners can schedule revaccinations by appointment with Kane County Animal Control. This situation highlights the importance of maintaining up-to-date rabies vaccinations for pets.
Original source text (English)

Since they may have lost potency, they have been recalled.The county emphasized that this does not mean an affected pet has rabies or is otherwise sick. (WWSB) - Sarasota County has issued a rabies advisory after an infected racoon was killed near Proctor Road Monday, the Department of Health announced.Officials said the single case of rabies was confirmed after the raccoon was killed on the corner of Grove Point Boulevard and Proctor Road on July 21. “All residents and visitors should be aware that rabies may be currently present in the wild animal population,” the advisory said.People and domestic animals should always avoid physical contact with wild animals including bats, foxes, skunks, otters, bobcats and coyotes, which carry a higher risk of human exposure. Rabies advisory issued in Charlotte County after raccoon tests positiveAnimals with rabies may show strange behavior, the health department says. Infected animals can be aggressive, attacking for no apparent reason, or act very tame (especially wild animals). They may not be able to eat, drink or swallow.They may drool because they are unable to swallow their saliva. They may stagger or become paralyzed. Eventually they will die.If you are exposed to rabies, receiving appropriate treatment after exposure will protect you from the risk of rabies.People can take precautions to prevent rabies exposure:Immunize pets and livestock based on your veterinarian’s recommended schedule.Keep pets under direct supervision and on a leash, and keep livestock secured on your property. If an animal bites your pet or livestock, seek veterinary assistance for the animal immediately and contact Sarasota Animal Services at 941-861-9501.Avoid contact with wild or stray animals. Do not handle, feed, or unintentionally attract them with outdoor pet food, open garbage cans, or other sources of food. If you have been bitten or scratched by a wild or domestic animal, seek medical attention, and report the injury to DOH-Sarasota by calling 941-861-2873.Never adopt wild animals or bring them into your home; instead contact a licensed wildlife rehabilitator.Call your local animal control agency to remove any stray animals from your neighborhood. Contact Sarasota Animal Services at 941-861-9501.Prevent wildlife, including bats, from entering living quarters or occupied spaces in homes, schools, and other similar areas where they might come in contact with people and pets.
