Rabid Bat Confirmed in Blaine County, Public Health Officials Advise Residents to Take Precautions - Outdoor Wire
新闻简报2026 年 7 月 14 日,中南部公共卫生区确认爱达荷州布莱恩县发现了一只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,这是爱达荷州中南部今年首例此类病例。狂犬病病毒 仅在爱达荷州的蝙蝠中发现,影响了其中约 1% 的蝙蝠,可通过咬伤或抓伤对人类和宠物致命。官员敦促居民避免接触蝙蝠,就潜在接触蝙蝠寻求医疗救助,并为宠物接种疫苗。提供了布莱恩县、莱姆希县和卡斯特县当地卫生区的联系信息。随着蝙蝠在秋季迁徙,遭遇可能会增加,这强调了对蝙蝠进行防护的房屋并为蝙蝠提供空间的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 22 日。事件日期:2026 年 7 月 14 日(蝙蝠确认患有狂犬病)。
地点与对象
布莱恩县,位于美国爱达荷州斯坦利以南。受影响人群:该地区的居民、宠物、野生动物。
事件
中南公共卫生区 (SCPHD) 报告确诊蝙蝠狂犬病病例。这是爱达荷州中南部今年首例确诊患有狂犬病的蝙蝠。
规模、影响与风险
单一确诊蝙蝠狂犬病病例。狂犬病 在爱达荷州蝙蝠中并不常见(约 1%)。该病毒可导致人和宠物致命的疾病。因蝙蝠咬伤或抓伤而存在暴露风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生官员建议采取预防措施,并提供检测联系信息/治疗。居民被告知避免接触蝙蝠,接触蝙蝠后就医,并给宠物接种疫苗。不确定性:没有关于正在进行的调查或其他案件的信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabid Bat Confirmed in Blaine County, Public Health Officials Advise Residents to Take Precautions - Outdoor Wire

News Brief
South Central Public Health District confirmed a rabid bat in Blaine County, Idaho, on July 14, 2026, marking the first such case in South Central Idaho this year. The rabies virus, found only in bats in Idaho and affecting about 1% of them, can be fatal to humans and pets through bites or scratches. Officials urge residents to avoid handling bats, seek medical attention for potential exposures, and vaccinate pets. Contact information is provided for local health districts in Blaine, Lemhi, and Custer counties. As bats migrate in fall, encounters may increase, emphasizing the need for bat-proofing homes and giving bats space.
Original source text (English)
Wednesday, July 22, 2026 | Wildlife Rabid Bat Confirmed in Blaine County, Public Health Officials Advise Residents to Take Precautions South Central Public Health District (SCPHD) recently confirmed rabies in a bat on July 14 located in Blaine County, south of Stanley. The case is the first confirmed rabid bat in South Central Idaho this year and serves as a reminder to take extra precautions should you encounter one. The rabies virus can cause a fatal illness in both people and their pets. Although rabies is found only in bats in Idaho, the disease is uncommon, occurring in only about 1% of bats. The virus is spread through the saliva, typically by a bite or scratch. Because it is difficult to determine whether a bat is infected based on its appearance or behavior, people should avoid handling bats and treat any direct contact as a potential exposure. If a person or pet may have been exposed to a live bat, contact your local public health district to determine whether rabies testing or medical treatment is needed. Residents in Lemhi and Custer counties should contact Eastern Idaho Public Health at (208) 533-3152. Blaine County residents should contact South Central Public Health District at (208) 737-5912. As bats begin migrating and preparing for hibernation in the fall, encounters with people and pets can become more common. Give bats plenty of space and avoid disturbing them. If you find a live bat inside your home or another enclosure, please contact the Salmon Regional Office for assistance on safe removal. To protect yourself and pets from rabies: Do not touch a bat with your bare hands. If you come in contact with a bat, seek medical attention. Place the bat in a ventilated (i.e., not airtight if the bat is alive) container while using thick gloves or another method to transfer into a container without touching it with bare skin. Contact your local public health district Always vaccinate your pets, including cats and horses. Pets may encounter bats outdoors or in the home. Bat-proof your home or cabin by plugging all holes in the siding and maintain tight fitting screens on windows. Bats can enter through holes the size of a quarter. To avoid killing dependent young inside the structure, wait to seal outside entrances until after bats are gone in the fall. Sealing gaps on the interior of the structure should be completed immediately and can be safely done anytime. For more information on bats and rabies please visit: Found a bat in your home?: https://idfg.idaho.gov/article/found-bat-your-home-dont-panic-here-are-steps-protect-yourself-and-bat The Idaho Department of Health and Welfare rabies site: https://healthandwelfare.idaho.gov/health-wellness/diseases-conditions/rabies
View five news elements

Publication date: July 22, 2026. Event date: July 14, 2026 (bat confirmed rabid).
Location and population
Blaine County, south of Stanley, Idaho, USA. Affected population: residents, pets, and wildlife in the area.
Event
Confirmed rabid bat case reported by South Central Public Health District (SCPHD). This is the first confirmed rabid bat in South Central Idaho this year.
Scale, impact and risk
Single confirmed rabid bat case. Rabies is uncommon in Idaho bats (about 1%). The virus can cause fatal illness in people and pets. Risk of exposure through bat bites or scratches.
Response, status and uncertainty
Public health officials advise precautions and provide contact information for testing/treatment. Residents are instructed to avoid handling bats, seek medical attention for exposures, and vaccinate pets. Uncertainty: No information on ongoing investigations or additional cases.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health district reports first rabid bat in Lake County since 2022 - News-Herald

News Brief
The Ohio Department of Health confirmed Lake County's first rabid bat since 2022, found in a Painesville house. Residents are advised to capture bats carefully and contact the health department for testing. Health officials emphasize mandatory rabies vaccinations for pets and warn of exposure risks through bites, scratches, or saliva contact. No human or animal cases have been reported. The Lake County General Health District is urging vigilance while reassuring residents that outdoor activities remain safe with proper precautions.
Original source text (English)
By Staff Report | editor@news-herald.com | Hartford Courant Staff PUBLISHED: July 24, 2026 at 3:45 PM EDT Getting your Trinity Audio player ready... The Ohio Department of Health recently confirmed Lake County’s first rabid bat since 2022, according to an announcement from the Lake County General Health District. The health district said that the bat was found in a Painesville house. “Humans and pets are exposed to the rabies virus when they are either bitten or scratched by an animal with rabies; or less commonly, when saliva from an infected animal gets into an open wound or mucous membrane,” said LCGHD Environmental Supervisor Bert Mechenbier in the online post. Mechenbier added that if a person is exposed to a bat, “or if a bat is discovered in the house while residents are sleeping,” the bat needs to be “carefully captured.” Residents should call the health department at 440-350-2543 to decide if the bat needs to be tested. “Residents should not fear spending time outside, but should be aware and keep their pets nearby,” said Health Commissioner Adam Litke in the post. Mechenbier added that there are mandatory rabies vaccination laws for dogs, cats and ferrets that are more than 4 months old, including indoor pets. He urged families to ensure the pet vaccinations are up to date. “Vaccinating pets against rabies protects the pet, as well as family members, against the threat of this fatal disease,” the post said. The health district said that more information about rabies can be found from the Ohio Department of Health at odh.ohio.gov or the Centers for Disease Control at cdc.gov/rabies . More From Our Newsroom

Scale, impact and risk
No cases or deaths reported. Risk of rabies virus exposure through bites, scratches, or saliva contact with open wounds or mucous membranes.
Response, status and uncertainty

新闻简报2026 年 7 月 22 日星期二,佐治亚州迪卡尔布县的卫生官员证实,哥伦比亚大道和约克广场附近的一只死蝙蝠检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性。石山地区的 Millstone Trail 附近还报告了一只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠。县当局发布公共警告,强调佐治亚州是 狂犬病 的地方病,该病毒全年在动物中自然传播。目前尚未有人类或宠物接触的报道,但官员敦促居民每年为宠物接种疫苗,保护垃圾桶,取消户外喂食站,并在被野生动物咬伤或抓伤时立即寻求医疗护理。动物控制中心提供了报告咬伤或请求援助的联系电话号码。

美国佐治亚州迪卡尔布县。特别是在迪卡尔布县南部的哥伦比亚大道和约克广场附近,以及石山地区的磨石步道附近。

在迪卡尔布县南部发现了确诊的 狂犬病 阳性死蝙蝠。县官员向公众发出警告。据报道,石山磨石步道附近还发现了一只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠。

没有人类或宠物接触的报道。佐治亚州是 狂犬病 的地方病,该病毒全年在动物中自然传播。没有提到人类或动物感染的直接病例。

卫生官员发布了公共警报。居民建议每年为宠物接种疫苗,保护垃圾桶,取消户外宠物喂食站,并在被咬或抓伤时立即就医。关于动物控制部门是否在当地进行诱捕,或者是否有人/宠物与蝙蝠有直接接触,存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dead bat tests positive for rabies in south DeKalb County - FOX 5 Atlanta

News Brief
Health officials in DeKalb County, Georgia, confirmed a dead bat tested positive for rabies near Columbia Drive and York Place on Tuesday, July 22, 2026. A separate rabid bat was also reported near Millstone Trail in the Stone Mountain area. County authorities issued public warnings, emphasizing that Georgia is endemic for rabies, with the virus circulating naturally among animals year-round. No human or pet exposure has been reported, but officials urged residents to vaccinate pets annually, secure garbage cans, eliminate outdoor feeding stations, and seek immediate medical care if bitten or scratched by wildlife. Animal control provided contact numbers for reporting bites or requesting assistance.

FOX 13 NewsOrlando News - FOX 35 OrlandoGainesville News - FOX 51 Gainesville River Flood Warning until SUN 4:10 PM EDT, Fulton County, Forsyth County 2 Flood Watch until SAT 7:00 PM CDT, Cleburne County Dead bat tests positive for rabies in south DeKalb County By FOX 5 Atlanta Digital Team FOX 5 Atlanta DeKalb County Published July 22, 2026 8:44 PM EDT Share Copy Link Email Facebook Twitter LinkedIn Reddit Published July 22, 2026 8:44 PM EDT The Brief A dead bat tested positive for rabies in DeKalb County after being found near Columbia Drive and York Place on Tuesday.County officials also flagged a separate rabid bat discovery near Millstone Trail in the nearby Stone Mountain area.Health workers urge residents to vaccinate pets annually and immediately seek medical care if bitten or scratched by wildlife. DEKALB COUNTY, Ga. - Health officials in Decatur issued a public warning after testing confirmed a dead bat carried rabies in southern DeKalb County on Tuesday. What we know: A dead bat recovered near Columbia Drive and York Place in south DeKalb County tested positive for rabies on Tuesday. County workers also reported an infected bat near Millstone Trail in the Stone Mountain area. Officials noted that Georgia is endemic for rabies, meaning the brain-damaging virus circulates naturally among animals all year. What we don't know: Authorities have not stated whether any people or household pets came into direct contact with either bat. It remains unclear if animal control officers have conducted local trappings in response to these cases. What you can do: Residents must vaccinate their pets every year and register the official tags directly with DeKalb County Animal Control. Officials advise homeowners to secure garbage cans, eliminate outdoor pet feeding stations, and keep a close eye on pets for unusual behavior. Anyone bitten or scratched by a wild or strange animal should seek emergency medical attention right away. To report an animal bite or request assistance, call DeKalb County Animal Control at 404-294-2996, extension 2, during weekday hours from 9 a.m. to 5 p.m., or dial 404-294-2519 after hours. The backstory: Rabies spreads through saliva, primarily when an infected animal bites another creature or a human. Because the virus occurs regularly across the state, local authorities only trap wild animals if a domestic pet or human suffers a bite or scratch. The Source: The information in this story was gathered from N. Wallen, Animal Enforcement Services Director, who issued official public health alerts through press documentation, as well as the DeKalb County Communications Department. 超出预测:狂犬病 和蚊媒疾病的风险 - 雅虎

Beyond the Forecast: The risk of rabies and mosquito-borne diseases - Yahoo
新闻简报2026 年 7 月 24 日,罗德岛州波塔基特报告称,两只浣熊的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性,同时该州在克兰斯顿的蚊子样本中确认了西尼罗河病毒。WPRI 12 气象学家 Steven Matregrano 讨论了这两起事件,其中包括州兽医 Scott Marshall 博士在 狂犬病 上的见解以及灭蚊协调员 Jannelle Couret 关于降低蚊媒疾病风险的见解。该报告强调了公众对 狂犬病 和蚊媒疾病的认识和预防措施,并概括了地点以说明社区一级的调查结果。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Beyond the Forecast: The risk of rabies and mosquito-borne diseases - Yahoo

News Brief
On July 24, 2026, Pawtucket, Rhode Island, reported two raccoons tested positive for rabies, while the state confirmed West Nile virus in a mosquito sample from Cranston. WPRI 12 Meteorologist Steven Matregrano discussed both incidents, featuring insights from Dr. Scott Marshall, the state veterinarian, on rabies and Jannelle Couret, a mosquito abatement coordinator, on reducing mosquito-borne illness risks. The report emphasizes public awareness and preventive measures for rabies and mosquito-borne diseases, with locations generalized to illustrate community-level findings.
Original source text (English)
EAST PROVIDENCE, R.I. (WPRI) — On Friday, July 24, the city of Pawtucket announced that two raccoons tested positive for rabies . Around the same time, the state of Rhode Island disclosed that West Nile virus was detected in a mosquito sample collected in Cranston . In this edition of Beyond the Forecast , 12 News Meteorologist Steven Matregrano goes in-depth on both topics. First, he speaks with Dr. Scott Marshall, Rhode Island's state veterinarian, about what rabies is, which species it can affect and what to do if you come across a wild animal. Dr. Jannelle Couret, a mosquito abatement coordinator with the R.I. Department of Environmental Management, then joins the show to explain how to reduce the risk of mosquito-borne illnesses and protect yourself against mosquito bites. LEARN MORE: EEE, West Nile virus: The symptoms and how to protect yourself Locations are not exact and are meant to illustrate findings by community. Confirmed rabies cases in two raccoons in Pawtucket and detection of West Nile virus in a mosquito sample in Cranston. Reported by the city of Pawtucket and the state of Rhode Island.
Scale, impact and risk
Two raccoons tested positive for rabies. West Nile virus detected in one mosquito sample. Risk of rabies and mosquito-borne illnesses discussed.

Scott Marshall, Rhode Island's state veterinarian, and Jannelle Couret, mosquito abatement coordinator, provided information on rabies and mosquito-borne disease prevention. Source limitation: Partial original body.
